"Never mind, Barker" the doctor said kindly; "if you were not psychic the man would have had no effect upon you at all. You only need training and development. And when you have learned to interpret these feelings and sensations better, you will feel no fear, but only a great sympathy."

"Yes, sir; thank you, sir!" And Barker bowed and made his escape, while Dr. Silence, an amused smile lurking about the corners of his mouth, made his way noiselessly down the passage and put his eye to the spyhole in the door of the green study.

===Chinese===
在巴克沉重的脚步声朝绿室去之后，紧跟着一阵极轻、几乎像在跳舞的步子；片刻之后，那仆人进来禀报说，那位先生已在候着了。他仍然脸色发白，举止紧张。

"别在意，巴克，"医生和蔼地说；"你若不是有通灵之感，那人根本不会对你有任何影响。你只需受些训练和开发就是了。等你学会更好地解读这些感觉和感受之后，你便不会再害怕，只会生出莫大的同情。"

"是，先生；多谢先生！"巴克一躬身，溜之大吉。而寂静医生，嘴角边藏着一丝好笑，悄无声息地沿着走廊走过去，把眼睛贴到了绿色书房门上的窥孔前。

===Original===
This spyhole was so placed that it commanded a view of almost the entire room, and, looking through it, the doctor saw a hat, gloves, and umbrella lying on a chair by the table, but searched at first in vain for their owner.

The windows were both closed and a brisk fire burned in the grate. There were various signs—signs intelligible at least to a keenly intuitive soul—that the room was occupied, yet so far as human beings were concerned, it was empty, utterly empty. No one sat in the chairs; no one stood on the mat before the fire; there was no sign even that a patient was anywhere close against the wall, examining the Bocklin reproductions—as patients so often did when they thought they were alone—and therefore rather difficult to see from the spyhole. Ordinarily speaking, there was no one in the room. It was undeniable.

===Chinese===
这窥孔的位置，恰可俯瞰几乎整间屋子；透过它望去，医生看见一张椅子靠在桌边，上头搁着一顶帽子、一双手套和一把伞，可他起先怎么也寻不见这些东西的主人。

两扇窗都紧闭着，炉栅里一团旺火烧得正欢。屋里有种种迹象——至少对一颗敏锐的通灵之心而言是明明白白的迹象——表明这里头有人；然而就活人而言，这里空空如也，空得彻底。没有谁坐在椅子里；没有谁站在炉前的毡垫上；连一点迹象都没有，说有个病人正贴墙站着，端详那几幅伯克林的复制品——病人们自以为独处时常这样做，因而不大容易从窥孔里看到。照常理说，这屋里一个人也没有。这是无可否认的。

===Original===
Yet Dr. Silence was quite well aware that a human being *was* in the room. His psychic apparatus never failed in letting him know the proximity of an incarnate or discarnate being. Even in the dark he could tell that. And he now knew positively that his patient—the patient who had alarmed Barker, and had then tripped down the corridor with that dancing footstep—was somewhere concealed within the four walls commanded by his spyhole. He also realised—and this was most unusual—that this individual whom he desired to watch knew that he was being watched. And, further, that the stranger himself was also watching! In fact, that it was he, the doctor, who was being observed—and by an observer as keen and trained as himself.

===Chinese===
然而寂静医生心知肚明，屋里*确确实实*有个人在。他那套通灵的机能，从不会在告知他一个具肉身的生灵或离体之灵临近之时失灵；纵在黑暗里他也分辨得出。此刻他确凿无疑地知道，他的病人——那个惊了巴克、随后又踏着那跳舞般的步子溜下走廊的病人——正藏在他窥孔所辖的那四面墙内的某处。他还觉察到——这可是极不寻常的——他想暗中观看的这个人，知道有人在看他。不仅如此，那陌生人自己也在看！事实上，被观察的正是他这位医生——而那观察者，和他一般敏锐、一般训练有素。

===Original===
An inkling of the true state of the case began to dawn upon him, and he was on the verge of entering—indeed, his hand already touched the doorknob—when his eye, still glued to the spyhole, detected a slight movement. Directly opposite, between him and the fireplace, something stirred. He watched very attentively and made certain that he was not mistaken. An object on the mantelpiece—it was a blue vase—disappeared from view. It passed out of sight together with the portion of the marble mantelpiece on which it rested. Next, that part of the fire and grate and brass fender immediately below it vanished entirely, as though a slice had been taken clean out of them.

Dr. Silence then understood that something between him and these objects was slowly coming into being, something that concealed them and obstructed his vision by inserting itself in the line of sight between them and himself.

He quietly awaited further results before going in.

===Chinese===
真相的一丝端倪开始在他心里浮现，他正要推门进去——手已搭上了门把手——忽然那只仍贴在窥孔上的眼睛察觉到一丝轻微的动静。正对面，在他与壁炉之间，有什么东西动了动。他凝神细看，确信自己没有眼花。壁炉架上的一件东西——那是一只蓝色的花瓶——从视野里消失了。它连同底下方才承着它的那一截大理石炉架，一同没了踪影。紧接着，它正下方的炉火、炉栅与黄铜围栏的那一部分，也整个儿消失了，仿佛被齐齐切去了一片。

寂静医生于是明白，在他与这些东西之间，正有某物缓缓成形，那东西遮住了它们、挡住了他的视线，因为它正插进他与它们之间的视线之中。

他静静等着看进一步的结果，才推门进去。

===Original===
First he saw a thin perpendicular line tracing itself from just above the height of the clock and continuing downwards till it reached the woolly fire-mat. This line grew wider, broadened, grew solid. It was no shadow; it was something substantial. It defined itself more and more. Then suddenly, at the top of the line, and about on a level with the face of the clock, he saw a round luminous disc gazing steadily at him. It was a human eye, looking straight into his own, pressed there against the spyhole. And it was bright with intelligence. Dr. Silence held his breath for a moment—and stared back at it.

===Chinese===
起先他看见一条细细的竖线自行描画出来，从钟的高度略上方起，一直向下延伸到那毛绒绒的炉前毡垫。这条线渐渐变宽、变粗、变得结实。那不是影子；那是某种实打实的东西。它把自己勾勒得越来越分明。忽然，在那线条的顶端、约莫与钟面齐平的地方，他看见一只圆圆的、发亮的圆盘定定地望着他。那是一只人的眼睛，正笔直地望进他的眼里，就贴在窥孔那头。那眼睛里闪烁着灵慧之光。寂静医生屏住片刻呼吸——回望着它。

===Original===
Then, like someone moving out of deep shadow into light, he saw the figure of a man come sliding sideways into view, a whitish face following the eye, and the perpendicular line he had first observed broadening out and developing into the complete figure of a human being. It was the patient. He had apparently been standing there in front of the fire all the time. A second eye had followed the first, and both of them stared steadily at the spyhole, sharply concentrated, yet with a sly twinkle of humour and amusement that made it impossible for the doctor to maintain his position any longer.

===Chinese===
接着，像一个人从浓影里挪进光中，他看见一个人的身形侧着滑进视野，一张发白的脸跟在那只眼睛后面，而方才他所见的那条竖线则越展越宽，渐渐长成一个完整的人形。正是那位病人。显然他一直就站在炉火前。第二只眼睛跟在第一只之后，两只眼睛都定定地盯着那窥孔，目光犀利而专注，却又带着一丝狡黠的、好笑的闪光，叫医生再也无法在那窥孔后待下去了。

===Original===
He opened the door and went in quickly. As he did so he noticed for the first time the sound of a German band coming in gaily through the open ventilators. In some intuitive, unaccountable fashion the music connected itself with the patient he was about to interview. This sort of prevision was not unfamiliar to him. It always explained itself later.

===Chinese===
他推开门，快步走了进去。一进门，他头一回注意到，一阵德国乐队的乐声正欢快地从敞开的通风口里飘进来。凭着某种直觉的、说不清道不明的方式，这乐声竟与行将接见的那位病人勾连在了一起。这类先见之兆于他并不陌生。它们日后总会自见分晓。

===Original===
The man, he saw, was of middle age and of very ordinary appearance; so ordinary, in fact, that he was difficult to describe—his only peculiarity being his extreme thinness. Pleasant—that is, good—vibrations issued from his atmosphere and met Dr. Silence as he advanced to greet him, yet vibrations alive with currents and discharges betraying the perturbed and disordered condition of his mind and brain. There was evidently something wholly out of the usual in the state of his thoughts. Yet, though strange, it was not altogether distressing; it was not the impression that the broken and violent atmosphere of the insane produces upon the mind. Dr. Silence realised in a flash that here was a case of absorbing interest that might require all his powers to handle properly.

===Chinese===
他看得出，此人正值中年，相貌极其寻常；寻常到竟难以描摹——他唯一的特别之处，便是瘦得过分。从他气场里散出的是和悦的——也就是善的——振动，迎上前来与寂静医生相会；可那振动里又满是暗流与放电，泄露出他心智与头脑那纷乱失序的状态。显然，他的思绪处于某种全然反常的境地。然而这事虽怪，却并不全然令人压抑；那绝非疯人破碎暴烈的气场施于人心上的那种印象。寂静医生一闪念间便明白，眼前这桩案子引人入胜到了极点，要妥善处置，恐需倾尽他全部的能耐。

===Original===
"I was watching you through my little peephole—as you saw," he began, with a pleasant smile, advancing to shake hands. "I find it of the greatest assistance sometimes—"

But the patient interrupted him at once. His voice was hurried and had odd, shrill changes in it, breaking from high to low in unexpected fashion. One moment it thundered, the next it almost squeaked.

===Chinese===
"我方才透过我的小窥孔在看你——你也看见了。"他带着和悦的笑意开口，一边上前握手。"我发现，这法子有时顶顶管用——"

可病人立时打断了他。他的语声急促，带着古怪的、尖利的起落，出人意料地从高处跌到低处。前一刻还如雷鸣，后一刻几乎成了尖啸。

===Original===
"I understand without explanation," he broke in rapidly. "You get the true note of a man in this way—when he thinks himself unobserved. I quite agree. Only, in my case, I fear, you saw very little. My case, as you of course grasp, Dr. Silence, is extremely peculiar, uncomfortably peculiar. Indeed, unless Sir William had positively assured me—"

"My friend has sent you to me," the doctor interrupted gravely, with a gentle note of authority, "and that is quite sufficient. Pray, be seated, Mr.—"

"Mudge—Racine Mudge," returned the other.

===Chinese===
"不必解释，我都明白。"他急急地插话。"用这法子，才能听出一个人的真声——当他自以为无人窥看之时。我完全赞同。只是就我而言，我怕是你没看见多少。我这个案子，寂静医生，您自然也掂量得出，是极其古怪的，古怪得令人难安。说真的，要不是威廉爵士再三向我保证——"

"我那位朋友既已将你荐到我这里，"医生神情庄重地打断他，语调里含着一丝温和的威严，"那便足矣。请坐吧，——先生怎么称呼？"

"马奇——雷辛·马奇。"那人答道。

===Original===
"Take this comfortable one, Mr. Mudge," leading him to the fixed chair, "and tell me your condition in your own way and at your own pace. My whole day is at your service if you require it."

Mr. Mudge moved towards the chair in question and then hesitated.

"You will promise me not to use the narcotic buttons," he said, before sitting down. "I do not need them. Also I ought to mention that anything you think of vividly will reach my mind. That is apparently part of my peculiar case." He sat down with a sigh and arranged his thin legs and body into a position of comfort. Evidently he was very sensitive to the thoughts of others, for the picture of the green buttons had only entered the doctor's mind for a second, yet the other had instantly snapped it up. Dr. Silence noticed, too, that Mr. Mudge held on tightly with both hands to the arms of the chair.

===Chinese===
"坐这把舒坦的，马奇先生。"他一面引他走向那把钉死的椅子，一面说，"照你自己的法子、你自己的步子，跟我讲讲你的情形吧。我这一整天都归你用，你要是需要的话。"

马奇先生朝那把椅子走去，却又迟疑起来。

"您得答应我，别用那些麻醉按钮。"他还没坐下便说。"我用不着它们。还有我该提一句，您脑子里想得真切的一切，都会传到我的心里。这显然是我这桩怪病的一部分。"他叹了口气坐下，把自己那双瘦腿和瘦身子挪了个舒坦的姿势。他显然对他人的念头极敏感，因为那绿色按钮的画面不过在医生脑海里一闪，对方便已立刻捕捉了去。寂静医生还留意到，马奇先生双手紧紧抓着椅子的扶手。

===Original===
"I'm rather glad the chair is nailed to the floor," he remarked, as he settled himself more comfortably. "It suits me admirably. The fact is—and this is my case in a nutshell—which is all that a doctor of your marvellous development requires—the fact is, Dr. Silence, I am a victim of Higher Space. That's what's the matter with me—Higher Space!"

The two looked at each other for a space in silence, the little patient holding tightly to the arms of the chair which "suited him admirably," and looking up with staring eyes, his atmosphere positively trembling with the waves of some unknown activity; while the doctor smiled kindly and sympathetically, and put his whole person as far as possible into the mental condition of the other.

"Higher Space," repeated Mr. Mudge, "that's what it is. Now, do you think you can help me with *that*?"

===Chinese===
"这椅子钉死在地板上，我倒蛮高兴。"他把身子挪得更舒坦些时说道。"它再合我意不过了。实情是——我这病一句话便能说尽——像您这样修为惊人的医生，听这一句也就够了——实情是，寂静医生，我是一个高维空间的受害者。我得的就是这个病——高维空间！"

两人默默对视了一阵，小小的病人紧紧攥着那把"再合他意不过"的椅子的扶手，睁大双眼仰望着，他的气场分明正随着某种未知活动的波涛而颤动；而医生则和蔼、同情地微笑着，将自己整个身心尽可能地调到对方的心境上去。

"高维空间，"马奇先生重复道，"就是这个。那么，您觉得*这个*您能帮上忙吗？"

===Original===
There was a pause during which the men's eyes steadily searched down below the surface of their respective personalities. Then Dr. Silence spoke.

"I am quite sure I can help," he answered quietly; "sympathy must always help, and suffering always owns my sympathy. I see you have suffered cruelly. You must tell me all about your case, and when I hear the gradual steps by which you reached this strange condition, I have no doubt I can be of assistance to you."

He drew a chair up beside his interlocutor and laid a hand on his shoulder for a moment. His whole being radiated kindness, intelligence, desire to help.

===Chinese===
这中间有一阵停顿，两人的目光都稳稳地探入彼此性情的表层之下。随后寂静医生开口了。

"我十分确信我能帮你。"他平静地答道，"同情之心总能助人，而苦痛之人向来得着我的同情。我看得出，你受了大苦。你得把你这桩案子一五一十都告诉我，等我听清你是如何一步步走到这等古怪境地的，我定能助你一臂之力。"

他在对方身旁拉过一把椅子坐下，片刻间把手搭上对方的肩头。他整个人都散发着善意、睿智和乐于相助之意。

===Original===
"For instance," he went on, "I feel sure it was the result of no mere chance that you became familiar with the terrors of what you term Higher Space; for Higher Space is no mere external measurement. It is, of course, a spiritual state, a spiritual condition, an inner development, and one that we must recognise as abnormal, since it is beyond the reach of the world at the present stage of evolution. Higher Space is a mythical state."

"Oh!" cried the other, rubbing his birdlike hands with pleasure, "the relief it is to be able to talk to someone who can understand! Of course what you say is the utter truth. And you are right that no mere chance led me to my present condition, but, on the other hand, prolonged and deliberate study. Yet chance in a sense now governs it. I mean, my entering the condition of Higher Space seems to depend upon the chance of this and that circumstance. For instance, the mere sound of that German band sent me off. Not that all music will do so, but certain sounds, certain vibrations, at once key me up to the requisite pitch, and off I go. Wagner's music always does it, and that band must have been playing a stray bit of Wagner. But I'll come to all that later. Only first, I must ask you to send away your man from the spyhole."

===Chinese===
"比方说，"他接着说，"我深信，你之所以会熟稔你所称的高维空间的种种可怖，绝非出于偶然；因为高维空间绝非单纯的外在度量。它自然是一种心灵的境界、一种心灵的状况、一种内在的开展，而且我们得承认它是反常的，因为它已超乎现下进化阶段这世人的所能触及。高维空间是一种近乎神话的境界。"

"啊！"对方欢喜地搓着他那双鸟爪般的手叫道，"能跟一个听得懂的人说上话，真是多大的宽慰！您说的自然是句句实话。您说得对，引我到今日这般田地的，并非偶然，反倒是长久而有意的钻研。然而从某种意义上说，如今倒是由偶然在主宰它了。我是说，我进入高维空间的状态，似乎全看这样那样境况的凑巧。比方说，方才那德国乐队一响，我就被送走了。倒不是说所有的音乐都能如此，而是某些声响、某些振动，能立时把我调到所需的音高，于是我便——去了。瓦格纳的音乐向来如此，那乐队方才奏的，必是一段零星的瓦格纳。这些我待会儿再说。只是头一件事，我得请您叫您那个人从窥孔前走开。"

===Original===
John Silence looked up with a start, for Mr. Mudge's back was to the door, and there was no mirror. He saw the brown eye of Barker glued to the little circle of glass, and he crossed the room without a word and snapped down the black shutter provided for the purpose, and then heard Barker snuffle away along the passage.

"Now," continued the little man in the chair, "I can begin. You have managed to put me completely at my ease, and I feel I may tell you my whole case without shame or reserve. You will understand. But you must be patient with me if I go into details that are already familiar to you—details of Higher Space, I mean—and if I seem stupid when I have to describe things that transcend the power of language and are really therefore indescribable."

"My dear friend," put in the other calmly, "that goes without saying. To know Higher Space is an experience that defies description, and one is obliged to make use of more or less intelligible symbols. But, pray, proceed. Your vivid thoughts will tell me more than your halting words."

===Chinese===
约翰·寂静猛地一抬头，因为马奇先生背对着门，而屋里并无镜子。他看见巴克那只褐色的眼睛正贴在那小小的玻璃圆孔上，便一言不发地穿过屋子，把专为遮挡而设的黑色小百叶"啪"地一合，随即听见巴克吸着鼻子沿走廊溜了开去。

"好了，"椅中的小个子接着说，"我可以开始了。您竟让我如此彻底地安下心来，我觉得自己尽可以毫无羞惭、毫无保留地把整桩案子讲给您听。您会明白的。只是您得对我耐心些，万一我讲些您早已熟稔的细枝末节——我是说关于高维空间的细枝末节——又或者，当我不得不去描绘那些超乎语言之力、因而实在无从描绘的事物时显得笨拙，还请见谅。"

"我亲爱的朋友，"对方平静地插话，"这是不消说的。识得高维空间，是一种无法言传的经历，人只好借用些多少能听懂的象征。但请讲下去吧。你那些真切的念头，会比你这磕磕巴巴的话语告诉我更多。"

===Original===
An immense sigh of relief proceeded from the little figure half lost in the depths of the chair. Such intelligent sympathy meeting him halfway was a new experience to him, and it touched his heart at once. He leaned back, relaxing his tight hold of the arms, and began in his thin, scale-like voice.

"My mother was a Frenchwoman, and my father an Essex bargeman," he said abruptly. "Hence my name—Racine and Mudge. My father died before I ever saw him. My mother inherited money from her Bordeaux relations, and when she died soon after, I was left alone with wealth and a strange freedom. I had no guardian, trustees, sisters, brothers, or any connection in the world to look after me. I grew up, therefore, utterly without education. This much was to my advantage; I learned none of that deceitful rubbish taught in schools, and so had nothing to unlearn when I awakened to my true love—mathematics, higher mathematics and higher geometry. These, however, I seemed to know instinctively. It was like the memory of what I had deeply studied before; the principles were in my blood, and I simply raced through the ordinary stages, and beyond, and then did the same with geometry. Afterwards, when I read the books on these subjects, I understood how swift and undeviating the knowledge had come back to me. It was simply memory. It was simply *recollecting* the memories of what I had known before in a previous existence and required no books to teach me."

===Chinese===
一声如释重负的长叹，从那半隐在椅子深处的小小身形里吐了出来。这般聪慧的同情，半道上来迎他，于他是头一遭的经历，顿时打动他的心。他往后一靠，松开紧攥扶手的手，用他那又细又像鳞片刮擦般的嗓音开了口。

"我母亲是法国人，我父亲是埃塞克斯的驳船工。"他突然说道，"我的名字便由此而来——雷辛与马奇。我父亲在我尚未见过他一面时便已去世。母亲从她波尔多的亲戚那里继承了一笔钱，不久她也撒手人寰，留下我一个人，有钱，又有一种古怪的自在。我没有监护人、没有受托人，没有姐妹、没有兄弟，世上没有任何亲人照看我。于是我便在毫无教育的境地里长大。这倒于我有利：学校里教的那些骗人的劳什子我一点没学，待我觉醒于我的真爱——数学，高等数学与高等几何——之时，便无须再去忘掉什么。然而这些学问，我仿佛是凭本能就懂的。那就像是我从前深研过的东西的记忆；那些原理就在我的血液里，我只是一路飞奔着穿过寻常的阶段，奔到更远之处，又对几何如法炮制。后来，等我读了这些科目的书，我才明白这知识回来得是何等迅疾、何等不偏不倚。那不过是记忆罢了。那不过是*追忆*起我在前生已然知晓的东西，根本无须书本相授。"

===Original===
In his growing excitement, Mr. Mudge attempted to drag the chair forward a little nearer to his listener, and then smiled faintly as he resigned himself instantly again to its immovability, and plunged anew into the recital of his singular "disease."

"The audacious speculations of Bolyai, the amazing theories of Gauss—that through a point more than one line could be drawn parallel to a given line; the possibility that the angles of a triangle are together *greater* than two right angles, if drawn upon immense curvatures—the breathless intuitions of Beltrami and Lobatchewsky—all these I hurried through, and emerged, panting but unsatisfied, upon the verge of my—my new world, my Higher Space possibilities—in a word, my disease!

===Chinese===
马奇先生越说越激动，想把椅子往前拖一点、离听者近些，随即便微微一笑，立时又认命于它的不可移动，重新一头扎进他那桩怪"病"的讲述里去。

"波尔约那些大胆的玄想，高斯那些惊人的理论——过一点可作不止一条直线与一给定直线平行；一个三角形的内角和，若画在极大的曲面上，可能合起来*大于*两个直角——贝尔特拉米与罗巴切夫斯基那些令人屏息的直觉——这一切我都匆匆奔过，气喘吁吁却不满足地，来到了我的——我的新世界的边缘，我的高维空间之诸般可能——一句话，我的病！

===Original===
"How I got there," he resumed after a brief pause, during which he appeared to be listening intently for an approaching sound, "is more than I can put intelligibly into words. I can only hope to leave your mind with an intuitive comprehension of the possibility of what I say.

"Here, however, came a change. At this point I was no longer absorbing the fruits of studies I had made before; it was the beginning of new efforts to learn for the first time, and I had to go slowly and laboriously through terrible work. Here I sought for the theories and speculations of others. But books were few and far between, and with the exception of one man—a 'dreamer,' the world called him—whose audacity and piercing intuition amazed and delighted me beyond description, I found no one to guide or help.

===Chinese===
"我是如何到达那里的，"他停了片刻又说——这中间他似乎在凝神倾听一个正逼近的声响——"这我可没法用言语说得分明。我唯有希望，能让您心里对我所言的可能性，生出一种直觉的领会。

"然而到了这里，情形变了。到了这一步，我便不再只是吸收往日所下功夫的果实；这是头一回要去学着下新功夫的开端，我只好缓慢而艰苦地熬过可怖的劳作。在这里，我寻觅他人的理论与玄想。可相关之书寥若晨星，除了一人——世人称之为'梦想家'——其胆识与洞穿一切的直觉令我惊叹喜悦到无以复加之外，我再找不到谁来引我、助我。

===Original===
"You, of course, Dr. Silence, understand something of what I am driving at with these stammering words, though you cannot perhaps yet guess what depths of pain my new knowledge brought me to, nor why an acquaintance with a new development of space should prove a source of misery and terror."

Mr. Racine Mudge, remembering that the chair would not move, did the next best thing he could in his desire to draw nearer to the attentive man facing him, and sat forward upon the very edge of the cushions, crossing his legs and gesticulating with both hands as though he saw into this region of new space he was attempting to describe, and might any moment tumble into it bodily from the edge of the chair and disappear from view. John Silence, separated from him by three paces, sat with his eyes fixed upon the thin white face opposite, noting every word and every gesture with deep attention.

===Chinese===
"您自然，寂静医生，从我这些结结巴巴的话语里明白了几分我所指的方向，尽管您或许还猜不到这新知识将我带入了多深的痛苦，也想不通何以识得空间的一种新开展，竟会沦为苦痛与惊惧之源。"

雷辛·马奇先生记起椅子挪不动，便尽其想凑近对面那位专注之人的渴望，做了件退而求其次的事——他坐到坐垫的最前沿，翘起二郎腿，双手比画着，仿佛他已望见他正竭力描绘的那片新空间之境，随时都会从椅子边上连人带身栽进去、没了踪影。约翰·寂静与他隔着三步之遥，坐着，目光定定地落在对面那张瘦白的脸上，一字一句、一举一停都看得入微。

===Original===
"This room we now sit in, Dr. Silence, has one side open to space—to Higher Space. A closed box only *seems* closed. There is a way in and out of a soap bubble without breaking the skin."

"You tell me no new thing," the doctor interposed gently.

"Hence, if Higher Space exists and our world borders upon it and lies partially in it, it follows necessarily that we see only portions of all objects. We never see their true and complete shape. We see their three measurements, but not their fourth. The new direction is concealed from us, and when I hold this book and move my hand all round it I have not really made a complete circuit. We only perceive those portions of any object which exist in our three dimensions; the rest escapes us. But, once we learn to see in Higher Space, objects will appear as they actually are. Only they will thus be hardly recognisable!

"Now, you may begin to grasp something of what I am coming to."

===Chinese===
"我们如今所坐的这间屋子，寂静医生，有一面对着空间——对着高维空间——是敞开的。一只合上的盒子，不过是*看似*合上罢了。不弄破表皮，也有法子进出一个肥皂泡。"

"你这话对我并非新鲜。"医生温和地插言。

"由此，若高维空间存在，而我们的世界与它接壤、且部分地置身其中，那么必然的结果便是：一切事物，我们都只看得见其中一部分。我们从未见过它们真实而完整的形状。我们看见它们的长宽高三维，却看不见那第四维。那新的方向对我们是隐没的，当我拿起一本书、把手沿它兜上一圈时，我其实并未真正绕满一整圈。任何事物，我们只觉察到存在于我们三维之内的那些部分；其余的尽从我们眼前溜走。可是一旦我们学会在高维空间里去看，事物便会显出它们本来的样子。只不过这样一来，它们便几乎认不出来了！

"现在，您大约开始能摸到我下面要说什么了。"

===Original===
"I am beginning to understand something of what you must have suffered," observed the doctor soothingly, "for I have made similar experiments myself, and only stopped just in time—"

"You are the one man in all the world who can hear and understand, *and* sympathise," exclaimed Mr. Mudge, grasping his hand and holding it tightly while he spoke. The nailed chair prevented further excitability.

"Well," he resumed, after a moment's pause, "I procured the implements and the coloured blocks for practical experiment, and I followed the instructions carefully till I had arrived at a working conception of four-dimensional space. The tessaract, the figure whose boundaries are cubes, I knew by heart. That is to say, I knew it and saw it mentally, for my eye, of course, could never take in a new measurement, or my hands and feet handle it.

"So, at least, I thought," he added, making a wry face. "I had reached the stage, you see, when I could imagine in a new dimension. I was able to conceive the shape of that new figure which is intrinsically different to all we know—the shape of the tessaract. I could perceive in four dimensions. When, therefore, I looked at a cube I could see all its sides at once. Its top was not foreshortened, nor its farther side and base invisible. I saw the whole thing out flat, so to speak. And this tessaract was bounded by cubes! Moreover, I also saw its content—its insides."

===Chinese===
"我开始明白几分你必定受过的苦了。"医生抚慰地说，"因为我自己也做过类似的实验，只是及时收了手——"

"您是这世上唯一能听、能懂，*又能*同情的人！"马奇先生叫道，一面抓住他的手紧紧握着，一面说。那钉死的椅子，阻止了他进一步的激动。

"嗯，"他停了片刻又接着说，"我弄来了器具和上了色的木块，着手实际操演，并仔细依着说明，直到我对四维空间有了一个能上手操作的概念。那超立方体——以立方体为界面的图形——我背得烂熟。也就是说，我心知其形、心见其形，因为我的眼睛，自然永远无法容纳一个新的维度，我的手脚也无法去把弄它。

"至少，我那时是这么想的。"他扮了个鬼脸，又补道。"您看，我已到了能在新的维度里去想象的阶段。我已能构想那个在本质上与我们一切所知截然不同的新图形的形状——那超立方体的形状。我能在四维里去感知。于是，当我看一只立方体时，我能一眼看见它所有的面。它的顶并不因透视而缩短，它的远侧和底也并非看不见。可以这么说，我把整件东西平平地看了个通透。而这一只超立方体，是以立方体为界的！不仅如此，我还看见了它的内涵——它的内里。"

===Original===
"You were not yourself able to enter this new world," interrupted Dr. Silence.

"Not then. I was only able to conceive intuitively what it was like and how exactly it must look. Later, when I slipped in there and saw objects in their entirety, unlimited by the paucity of our poor three measurements, I very nearly lost my life. For, you see, space does not stop at a single new dimension, a fourth. It extends in all possible new ones, and we must conceive it as containing any number of new dimensions. In other words, there is no space at all, but only a spiritual condition. But, meanwhile, I had come to grasp the strange fact that the objects in our normal world appear to us only partially."

===Chinese===
"你自己还没能走进这个新世界。"寂静医生插言道。

"那时还不能。我只不过能凭直觉构想它是什么模样、它确切的样貌该当如何。后来，当我滑进了那里，看见事物那不受我们可怜三维之贫乏所限的、完整的全貌之时，我险些丢了性命。因为您看，空间并不止于一个新维度、不止于第四维。它向一切可能的新维度延伸，我们得把它设想成包容着任意多个新维度。换句话说，根本无所谓空间，唯有心灵的境界而已。但与此同时，我已领会到那桩古怪的事实：我们寻常世界里的种种事物，在我们看来只是部分的呈现。"

===Original===
Mr. Mudge moved farther forward till he was balanced dangerously on the very edge of the chair. "From this starting point," he resumed, "I began my studies and experiments, and continued them for years. I had money, and I was without friends. I lived in solitude and experimented. My intellect, of course, had little part in the work, for intellectually it was all unthinkable. Never was the limitation of mere reason more plainly demonstrated. It was mystically, intuitively, spiritually that I began to advance. And what I learnt, and knew, and did is all impossible to put into language, since it all describes experiences transcending the experiences of men. It is only some of the results—what you would call the symptoms of my disease—that I can give you, and even these must often appear absurd contradictions and impossible paradoxes.

"I can only tell you, Dr. Silence"—his manner became exceedingly impressive—"that I reached sometimes a point of view whence all the great puzzle of the world became plain to me, and I understood what they call in the Yoga books 'The Great Heresy of Separateness'; why all great teachers have urged the necessity of man loving his neighbour as himself; how men are all really one; and why the utter loss of self is necessary to salvation and the discovery of the true life of the soul."

===Chinese===
马奇先生又往前挪了挪，挪到险险地坐在椅子最边上。"从这一起点出发，"他接着说，"我开始了我多年的钻研与实验，年复一年。我有钱，又没有朋友。我独居，操演。在这其中，我的理智自然所起的作用甚微，因为就理智而言，这一切都无从设想。纯粹的理性所受的限制，从未被揭示得如此彰明。我是从神秘、直觉、心灵之上开始向前迈步的。而我所学、所知、所行的一切，全都没法诉诸语言，因为它们描绘的全是超乎世人经验的经历。我能给您的，只不过是其中一些结果——也就是您会称作我那病之症候的东西——而且即便这些，也往往必显得是荒唐的自相矛盾、是不可能的悖论。

"我只能告诉您，寂静医生——"他的神态变得极有分量——"我有时会到达一个视角，从那里望去，这世界一切的大谜团于我都豁然开朗，我明白了瑜伽经典里所谓的'分离之大异端'；明白了何以一切大导师都力劝人须爱人如己；明白了人原来本是一体；又何以自我的彻底丧失，乃得救与发现灵魂真生命之所必需。"

===Original===
He paused a moment and drew breath.

"Your speculations have been my own long ago," the doctor said quietly. "I fully realise the force of your words. Men are doubtless not separate at all—in the sense they imagine—"

"All this about the very much Higher Space I only dimly, very dimly, conceived, of course," the other went on, raising his voice again by jerks; "but what did happen to me was the humbler accident of—the simpler disaster—oh, dear, how shall I put it—?"

He stammered and showed visible signs of distress.

"It was simply this," he resumed with a sudden rush of words, "that, accidentally, as the result of my years of experiment, I one day slipped bodily into the next world, the world of four dimensions, yet without knowing precisely how I got there, or how I could get back again. I discovered, that is, that my ordinary three-dimensional body was but an expression—a projection—of my higher four-dimensional body!

===Chinese===
他停了停，喘了口气。

"你的这些玄想，早就是我自己的了。"医生平静地说。"我完全体会得到你这些话的分量。人无疑根本不是彼此分离的——并非他们所想象的那种分离——"

"这一切关于那更高得多的高维空间的事，我当然只是隐约地、十分隐约地构想过，"对方接着说，声音又一跳一跳地高了起来；"可真正发生在我身上的，是那桩较卑微的意外——那较简单的灾祸——哎呀，天哪，我该怎么说才好——？"

他结巴起来，脸上明显露出痛苦之色。

"其实简单说来是这样，"他忽然一串话涌了出来，"作为我多年实验的结果，我竟在某一天连人带身地滑进了下一个世界，滑进了四维的世界，却又说不清自己是怎样到达那里的、又怎样能再回去。也就是说，我发现我寻常的三维肉身，不过是我那更高的四维之身的一种表达——一种投影！

===Original===
"Now you understand what I meant much earlier in our talk when I spoke of chance. I cannot control my entrance or exit. Certain people, certain human atmospheres, certain wandering forces, thoughts, desires even—the radiations of certain combinations of colour, and above all, the vibrations of certain kinds of music, will suddenly throw me into a state of what I can only describe as an intense and terrific inner vibration—and behold I am off! Off in the direction at right angles to all our known directions! Off in the direction the cube takes when it begins to trace the outlines of the new figure! Off into my breathless and semi-divine Higher Space! Off, *inside myself*, into the world of four dimensions!"

He gasped and dropped back into the depths of the immovable chair.

"And there," he whispered, his voice issuing from among the cushions, "there I have to stay until these vibrations subside, or until they do something which I cannot find words to describe properly or intelligibly to you—and then, behold, I am back again. First, that is, I disappear. Then I reappear."

===Chinese===
"现在您明白，我们谈的话一开始我所说的偶然是什么意思了吧。我控制不了我的出入。某些人、某些人的气场、某些游荡的力量、念头、甚至欲望——某些色彩组合的辐射，尤其是，某些音乐的振动，会猛地将我抛入一种我只能形容为剧烈而骇人的内在震颤的状态——你看，我就此去了！朝着与我们所知一切方向皆成直角的方向而去！朝着立方体开始描画那新图形轮廓时所取的方向而去！朝着我那令人屏息的、半神半圣的高维空间而去！朝着，*在我自身之内*，朝着那四维的世界而去！"

他倒抽一口气，跌回那不可移动的椅子的深处。

"在那里，"他低语道，声音从靠垫之间传出，"我只好待下去，直到这些振动平息，或者直到它们作了某件我没法向您妥帖而分明地形容的事——而后，你看，我又回来了。也就是说，我先消失。然后我又重现。"

===Original===
"Just so," exclaimed Dr. Silence, "and that is why a few—"

"Why a few moments ago," interrupted Mr. Mudge, taking the words out of his mouth, "you found me gone, and then saw me return. The music of that wretched German band sent me off. Your intense thinking about me brought me back—when the band had stopped its Wagner. I saw you approach the peephole and I saw Barker's intention of doing so later. For me no interiors are hidden. I see inside. When in that state the content of your mind, as of your body, is open to me as the day. Oh, dear, oh, dear, oh, dear!"

Mr. Mudge stopped and again mopped his brow. A light trembling ran over the surface of his small body like wind over grass. He still held tightly to the arms of the chair.

===Chinese===
"正是如此，"寂静医生叫道，"这便是何以方才——"

"何以方才片刻之前，"马奇先生从他嘴里抢过话头，"您发现我没了，又看见我回来。那倒霉德国乐队的乐声把我送走的。您对我那一阵用力的想念又把我带了回来——待到那乐队停了它的瓦格纳。我看见您走向窥孔，也看见巴克随后想去看的念头。于我而言，没有什么内里是藏得住的。我能看见内里。当处于那状态之时，您的心，正如您的身，其内涵对我敞开如白昼。唉，天哪，唉，天哪，唉，天哪！"

马奇先生停下来，又抹了一把额头。一阵轻微的颤栗掠过他那小小的身躯表面，像风掠过青草。他仍紧紧抓着椅子的扶手。

===Original===
"At first," he presently resumed, "my new experiences were so vividly interesting that I felt no alarm. There was no room for it. The alarm came a little later."

"Then you actually penetrated far enough into that state to experience yourself as a normal portion of it?" asked the doctor, leaning forward, deeply interested.

Mr. Mudge nodded a perspiring face in reply.

"I did," he whispered, "undoubtedly I did. I am coming to all that. It began first at night, when I realised that sleep brought no loss of consciousness—"

"The spirit, of course, can never sleep. Only the body becomes unconscious," interposed John Silence.

===Chinese===
"起初，"他过了一会儿又往下说，"我这些新经历鲜明有趣得很，我一点也没觉得惊慌。根本顾不上。惊慌是稍后才来的。"

"这么说，你当真深入那境界到足以把自身当作其中寻常一份子来体验了？"医生问，身子前倾，深感兴趣。

马奇先生点了点那张冒着汗的脸，权作回答。

"我体验过，"他低语，"确凿无疑地体验过。我这就说到那里。这事起初发生在夜里，那时我发觉，睡眠并未带来意识的失落——"

"心灵，自然是从不入眠的。失去知觉的只是肉身。"约翰·寂静插话。

===Original===
"Yes, we know that—theoretically. At night, of course, the spirit is active elsewhere, and we have no memory of where and how, simply because the brain stays behind and receives no record. But I found that, while remaining conscious, I also retained memory. I had attained to the state of continuous consciousness, for at night I regularly, with the first approaches of drowsiness, entered *nolens volens* the four-dimensional world.

"For a time this happened regularly, and I could not control it; though later I found a way to regulate it better. Apparently sleep is unnecessary in the higher—the four-dimensional—body. Yes, perhaps. But I should infinitely have preferred dull sleep to the knowledge. For, unable to control my movements, I wandered to and fro, attracted, owing to my partial development and premature arrival, to parts of this new world that alarmed me more and more. It was the awful waste and drift of a monstrous world, so utterly different to all we know and see that I cannot even hint at the nature of the sights and objects and beings in it. More than that, I cannot even remember them. I cannot now picture them to myself even, but can recall only the *memory of the impression* they made upon me, the horror and devastating terror of it all. To be in several places at once, for instance—"

"Perfectly," interrupted John Silence, noticing the increase of the other's excitement, "I understand exactly. But now, please, tell me a little more of this alarm you experienced, and how it affected you."

===Chinese===
"是，这个我们知道——理论上知道。夜里，心灵自然在别处活跃，而我们之所以不记得身在何处、所为何事，不过是因为头脑被留了下来、收不到任何记录。可我却发觉，在保持清醒的同时，我也保住了记忆。我已达到连续意识的境地，因为夜里，每当头一阵困倦袭来，我便不论情愿与否地、身不由己地，进入那四维的世界。

"有一阵子这事夜夜如此，我无法控制；不过后来我找到了一种更好地加以调控的法子。在那更高的——那四维的——身体里，睡眠似乎并不必要。也许是吧。可我宁愿千百倍地换取那酣沉的睡眠，也不愿换得这知识。因为我既无法掌控自己的行止，便只能来回游荡，又因我的半成之姿、过早到来之故，被吸引到这新世界里那些令我越发惊恐的地带。那是一个庞然的世界，其可怖的荒芜与漂流，与我们一切所见所知全然不同，我甚至无法对其中那些景象、物事和生灵的性质稍作暗示。不止如此，我甚至连它们都记不住。我此刻连在心里把它们浮现出来都做不到，所能追忆的，只有它们*在我心上留下的印象*——那一切的恐怖与毁灭性的惊惧。比如说，同时身处数地——"

"完全明白。"约翰·寂静注意到对方越发激动，便插言道，"我完全懂得。可现在，请你再跟我多讲一点你所经历的这份惊慌，以及它如何影响了你。"

===Original===
"It's not the disappearing and reappearing *per se* that I mind," continued Mr. Mudge, "so much as certain other things. It's seeing people and objects in their weird entirety, in their true and complete shapes, that is so distressing. It introduces me to a world of monsters. Horses, dogs, cats, all of which I loved; people, trees, children; all that I have considered beautiful in life—everything, from a human face to a cathedral—appear to me in a different shape and aspect to all I have known before. I cannot perhaps convince you why this should be terrible, but I assure you that it is so. To hear the human voice proceeding from this novel appearance which I scarcely recognise as a human body is ghastly, simply ghastly. To see inside everything and everybody is a form of insight peculiarly distressing. To be so confused in geography as to find myself one moment at the North Pole, and the next at Clapham Junction—or possibly at both places simultaneously—is absurdly terrifying. Your imagination will readily furnish other details without my multiplying my experiences now. But you have no idea what it all means, and how I suffer."

Mr. Mudge paused in his panting account and lay back in his chair. He still held tightly to the arms as though they could keep him in the world of sanity and three measurements, and only now and again released his left hand in order to mop his face. He looked very thin and white and oddly unsubstantial, and he stared about him as though he saw into this other space he had been talking about.

===Chinese===
"我所在意的，倒不是那消失与重现本身，"马奇先生接着说，"而是另外一些事。是看见人与物那怪诞的全体、那真实而完整的形状，才这般令人难堪。它把我引入了一个怪物的世界。马、狗、猫，这些我素来所爱的；人、树、孩童；一切我在生命中曾视作美的——从一张人脸到一座大教堂，每一件——在我眼里都呈现出与我从前所知全然不同的形状和面貌。我也许无法说服您这为何可怖，可我向您保证，它确是如此。听见人声从这副我几乎认不出是人身的崭新模样里发出，是可怖的，简直可怖。能看穿一切物、一切人的内里，是这样一种分外折磨人的洞见。在地理想象上混乱到这一刻还在北极、下一刻就到了克拉珀姆交汇站——又或者可能同时身处两地——是荒唐得令人惊惧的。其余的细节，您的想象力尽可轻易补足，无须我在此一一罗列我的经历。可您不知道这一切意味着什么，不知道我怎样受苦。"

马奇先生在他气喘吁吁的讲述里停了下来，靠回椅子里。他仍紧紧攥着扶手，仿佛它们能把他留在这清醒而三维的世界里，只是偶或松开左手去抹一把脸。他看上去极瘦、极白，古怪地虚渺不实，他四下张望着，仿佛已望进他所讲的那个别样的空间里去了。

===Original===
John Silence, too, felt warm. He had listened to every word and had made many notes. The presence of this man had an exhilarating effect upon him. It seemed as if Mr. Racine Mudge still carried about with him something of that breathless Higher-Space condition he had been describing. At any rate, Dr. Silence had himself advanced sufficiently far along the legitimate paths of spiritual and psychic transformations to realise that the visions of this extraordinary little person had a basis of truth for their origin.

After a pause that prolonged itself into minutes, he crossed the room and unlocked a drawer in a bookcase, taking out a small book with a red cover. It had a lock to it, and he produced a key out of his pocket and proceeded to open the covers. The bright eyes of Mr. Mudge never left him for a single second.

===Chinese===
约翰·寂静也觉得身上发热。他字字句句都听了进去，作了许多笔记。这人的在场，对他有一种振奋之效。仿佛雷辛·马奇先生身上至今仍携着些许他所描绘的那种令人屏息的高维空间之境。无论如何，寂静医生自己在心灵与通灵蜕变的正途上，也已走得够远，深知这位非凡小人物的种种幻视，其来有自，并非无根之木。

经过一阵延长到好几分钟的停顿之后，他穿过屋子，打开一只书柜的抽屉，取出一本红封面的小书。那书带着锁，他从口袋里掏出一把钥匙，将书封打开。马奇先生那双亮眼睛，一秒钟也没离开过他。

===Original===
"It almost seems a pity," he said at length, "to cure you, Mr. Mudge. You are on the way to discovery of great things. Though you may lose your life in the process—that is, your life here in the world of three dimensions—you would lose thereby nothing of great value—you will pardon my apparent rudeness, I know—and you might gain what is infinitely greater. Your suffering, of course, lies in the fact that you alternate between the two worlds and are never wholly in one or the other. Also, I rather imagine, though I cannot be certain of this from any personal experiments, that you have here and there penetrated even into space of more than four dimensions, and have hence experienced the terror you speak of."

The perspiring son of the Essex bargeman and the woman of Normandy bent his head several times in assent, but uttered no word in reply.

===Chinese===
"治愈您，马奇先生，几乎令人惋惜。"他终于说道。"您正走在发现伟大事物的道上。尽管您也许会在此过程中失了性命——也就是说，失了您在这三维世界里的性命——可您由此所失的并无多大价值——我知道您会原谅我这话里的冒昧——而您也许会得到那远为宏大的东西。您的苦痛，自然在于您往返于两个世界之间，从未全然身处其一。还有，我多少猜想——虽说我不能凭任何亲身实验来断定这一点——您这里那里，甚至已深入过四维以上的空间，因而经历了您所说的那份惊惧。"

这位埃塞克斯驳船工与诺曼底女子所生的、汗涔涔的儿子，连点了几下头表示赞同，却未置一词作答。

===Original===
"Some strange psychic predisposition, dating no doubt from one of your former lives, has favoured the development of your 'disease'; and the fact that you had no normal training at school or college, no leading by the poor intellect into the culs-de-sac falsely called knowledge, has further caused your exceedingly rapid movement along the lines of direct inner experience. None of the knowledge you have foreshadowed has come to you through the senses, of course."

Mr. Mudge, sitting in his immovable chair, began to tremble slightly. A wind again seemed to pass over his surface and again to set it curiously in motion like a field of grass.

"You are merely talking to gain time," he said hurriedly, in a shaking voice. "This thinking aloud delays us. I see ahead what you are coming to, only please be quick, for something is going to happen. A band is again coming down the street, and if it plays—if it plays Wagner—I shall be off in a twinkling."

===Chinese===
"某种古怪的通灵禀赋，无疑可追溯到您的前生之一，助长了您这'病'的滋长；而您未曾在中学或大学受过正规的训练、未曾被那可怜的理智领进那些被谬称为知识的死胡同，这一点又进一步促使您沿着直接内在体验的路线飞快地前行。您所预示的那一切知识，当然没有一丝一毫是经由感官来到您这里的。"

马奇先生坐在那不可移动的椅子里，开始微微发抖。仿佛又有一阵风掠过他的表面，又一次奇异地使它波动起来，如同一片草地。

"您不过是在拖延时间。"他声音发抖、急急地说。"您这样把心里话念出来，耽误了我们。我看得到您下面要说什么，只是请您快些，因为要有事发生了。又有一支乐队正沿街而来，它要是奏起来——要是奏起瓦格纳——我眨眼间就要去了。"

===Original===
"Precisely. I will be quick. I was leading up to the point of how to effect your cure. The way is this: You must simply learn to *block the entrances*."

"True, true, utterly true!" exclaimed the little man, dodging about nervously in the depths of the chair. "But how, in the name of space, is that to be done?"

"By concentration. They are all within you, these entrances, although outer cases such as colour, music and other things lead you towards them. These external things you cannot hope to destroy, but once the entrances are blocked, they will lead you only to bricked walls and closed channels. You will no longer be able to find the way."

"Quick, quick!" cried the bobbing figure in the chair. "How is this concentration to be effected?"

"This little book," continued Dr. Silence calmly, "will explain to you the way." He tapped the cover. "Let me now read out to you certain simple instructions, composed, as I see you divine, entirely from my own personal experiences in the same direction. Follow these instructions and you will no longer enter the state of Higher Space. The entrances will be blocked effectively."

Mr. Mudge sat bolt upright in his chair to listen, and John Silence cleared his throat and began to read slowly in a very distinct voice.

===Chinese===
"正是。我这就快些。我正要说到如何着手治您这病。法子是这样的：您只须学会*堵住那些入口*。"

"对，对，千真万确！"那小个子叫道，在椅子深处紧张地躲来闪去。"可看在空间的份上，这要怎么做到？"

"靠凝神定念。那些入口，全在您的内里，尽管色彩、音乐之类的外在事物会引您走向它们。这些外在之物您无从指望将之毁去，可那些入口一旦堵上，它们便只能把您引到砖墙之前、封闭的甬道之中。您将再也找不到那条路。"

"快，快！"椅子里那颠动不止的身影叫道。"这凝神定念要怎么做到？"

"这本小书，"寂静医生平静地接着说，"会向您讲明法子。"他敲了敲封皮。"现在让我给您读几条简单的指引——我看出您已猜到——这些全是我本人在同一方向上的亲身经历所写成的。照这些指引去做，您便不会再进入高维空间的状态。那些入口将被有效地堵上。"

马奇先生在椅子里挺直了腰板来听，约翰·寂静清了清嗓子，开始用十分清晰的嗓音慢慢地读起来。

===Original===
But before he had uttered a dozen words, something happened. A sound of street music entered the room through the open ventilators, for a band had begun to play in the stable mews at the back of the house—the March from *Tannhäuser*. Odd as it may seem that a German band should twice within the space of an hour enter the same mews and play Wagner, it was nevertheless the fact.

Mr. Racine Mudge heard it. He uttered a sharp, squeaking cry and twisted his arms with nervous energy round the chair. A piteous look that was not far from tears spread over his white face. Grey shadows followed it—the grey of fear. He began to struggle convulsively.

"Hold me fast! Catch me! For God's sake, keep me here! I'm on the rush already. Oh, it's frightful!" he cried in tones of anguish, his voice as thin as a reed.

===Chinese===
可他还没念出十二个字，事情就发生了。一阵街头乐声从敞开的通风口飘进了屋里，因为屋后那马厩巷里，一支乐队已开始演奏——《唐豪瑟》的进行曲。虽说一支德国乐队竟在一小时之内两度走进同一条马厩巷、两度奏起瓦格纳，听来未免离奇，可这确是事实。

雷辛·马奇先生听见了。他发出一声尖锐刺耳的惊叫，两条胳膊神经质地使劲绞住椅子。一张凄楚的、几乎要落泪的神情在他发白的脸上漫开来。灰色的阴影随之而来——那是恐惧的灰。他开始抽搐般地挣扎。

"抓紧我！抓住我！看在上帝份上，把我留在这儿！我已在飞冲之中了。哦，太可怕了！"他痛苦的声调里叫道，声音细如芦管。

===Original===
Dr. Silence made a plunge forward to seize him, but in a flash, before he could cover the space between them, Mr. Racine Mudge, screaming and struggling, seemed to shoot past him into invisibility. He disappeared like an arrow from a bow propelled at infinite speed, and his voice no longer sounded in the external air, but seemed in some curious way to make itself heard somewhere within the depths of the doctor's own being. It was almost like a faint singing cry in his head, like a voice of dream, a voice of vision and unreality.

"Alcohol, alcohol!" it cried, "give me alcohol! It's the quickest way. Alcohol, before I'm out of reach!"

The doctor, accustomed to rapid decisions and even more rapid action, remembered that a brandy flask stood upon the mantelpiece, and in less than a second he had seized it and was holding it out towards the space above the chair recently occupied by the visible Mudge. Then, before his very eyes, and long ere he could unscrew the metal stopper, he saw the contents of the closed glass phial sink and lessen as though someone were drinking violently and greedily of the liquor within.

"Thanks! Enough! It deadens the vibrations!" cried the faint voice in his interior, as he withdrew the flask and set it back upon the mantelpiece. He understood that in Mudge's present condition one side of the flask was open to space and he could drink without removing the stopper. He could hardly have had a more interesting proof of what he had been hearing described at such length.

===Chinese===
寂静医生一个箭步上前去抓他，可一闪之间，还没等他越过两人之间的距离，雷辛·马奇先生便尖叫着、挣扎着，仿佛从他身旁射了过去，射入了无形。他像一支以无限之速射出的箭那样消失了，而他的声音不再响在外面的空气里，却仿佛以某种古怪的方式，在医生自己生命的深处某处响了起来。那几乎像他脑中一声隐约的、歌唱般的呼喊，像梦中的声音，像幻象与虚妄之声。

"酒，酒！"那声音叫道，"给我酒！这是最快的法子。酒，趁我还够得着！"

这位医生素来惯于当机立断，行事更比决断还快，他记起壁炉架上搁着一只白兰地酒瓶，不出一秒便已抓在手中，朝那方才尚有那看得见的马奇所坐的椅子上方的空间伸去。随即，就在他眼前，还没等他拧开那金属瓶塞，他便看见那只封口的玻璃瓶里，液面下沉、见少，仿佛有什么人正凶猛而贪婪地大口吞饮着里面的酒。

"谢了！够了！它麻痹了那些振动！"他体内那隐约的声音叫道，他随即把酒瓶收回，放回壁炉架上。他明白了，在马奇眼下的状态里，那瓶子有一面对空间是敞开的，他尽可不拔瓶塞便喝到酒。他方才听了那么长一大段描绘，要找印证，再没有比这更有趣的了。

===Original===
But the next moment—the very same moment it almost seemed—the German band stopped midway in its tune—and there was Mr. Mudge back in his chair again, gasping and panting!

"Quick!" he shrieked, "stop that band! Send it away! Catch hold of me! Block the entrances! Block the entrances! Give me the red book! Oh, oh, oh‑h‑h‑h!!!"

The music had begun again. It was merely a temporary interruption. The *Tannhäuser* March started again, this time at a tremendous pace that made it sound like a rapid two-step as though the instruments played against time.

But the brief interruption gave Dr. Silence a moment in which to collect his scattering thoughts, and before the band had got through half a bar, he had flung forward upon the chair and held Mr. Racine Mudge, the struggling little victim of Higher Space, in a grip of iron. His arms went all round his diminutive person, taking in a good part of the chair at the same time. He was not a big man, yet he seemed to smother Mudge completely.

===Chinese===
可下一刻——几乎像是同一刻——那德国乐队奏到半句停了下来——而马奇先生已回到了他的椅子上，气喘吁吁！

"快！"他尖叫，"叫那乐队停下！叫他们走！抓住我！堵上那些入口！堵上那些入口！把那本红书给我！哦，哦，哦——呀——！！！"

乐声又响了起来。方才不过是一时中断。《唐豪瑟》进行曲重新奏起，这回快到了惊人的速度，听起来像一支急促的两步舞曲，仿佛那些乐器正与时间赛跑。

可这短短的中断给了寂静医生一个收拢四散思绪的空当，乐队还没奏完半小节，他便已扑到椅前，将雷辛·马奇这位挣扎不已的高维空间小小受害者，铁钳般地攥住。他的双臂把那矮小的身形整个儿兜住，连带着也搂过了椅子的一大半。他本人并非魁梧之躯，却仿佛将马奇整个儿罩没了。

===Original===
Yet, even as he did so, and felt the wriggling form underneath him, it began to melt and slip away like air or water. The wood of the armchair somehow disentangled itself from between his own arms and those of Mudge. The phenomenon known as the passage of matter through matter took place. The little man seemed actually to get mixed up in his own being. Dr. Silence could just see his face beneath him. It puckered and grew dark as though from some great internal effort. He heard the thin, reedy voice cry in his ear to "Block the entrances, block the entrances!" and then—but how in the world describe what is indescribable?

John Silence half rose up to watch. Racine Mudge, his face distorted beyond all recognition, was making a marvellous inward movement, as though doubling back upon himself. He turned funnel-wise like water in a whirling vortex, and then appeared to break up somewhat as a reflection breaks up and divides in a distorting convex mirror. He went neither forward nor backwards, neither to the right nor the left, neither up nor down. But he went. He went utterly. He simply flashed away out of sight like a vanishing projectile.

===Chinese===
然而，即便在他这样做、感觉到身下那扭动身形的同时，那身形已开始融化、滑脱，像空气，像水。那扶手椅的木头，竟不知怎地从他自己的胳膊与马奇的胳膊之间挣脱了出去。那被称为穿物——物质穿过物质——的现象发生了。这小个子仿佛真的在他自身的存在里搅作了一团。寂静医生勉强看得见他身下那张脸。那张脸皱缩着、发暗，仿佛出于某种巨大的内在用力。他听见那又细又像芦管的嗓音在他耳边喊"堵上那些入口，堵上那些入口！"接着——可这叫人怎么去描绘那无从描绘的事？

约翰·寂静半直起身来看。雷辛·马奇，那张脸扭曲得判若两人，正作着一个奇妙的向内的动作，仿佛朝他自己身上折了回去。他如旋涡里的水一般，漏斗似的打着转，接着仿佛破碎开来，有点像映像在变形的凸面镜里破碎、分裂。他既不向前，也不向后；既不向右，也不向左；既不向上，也不向下。可他去了。他去得彻彻底底。他就像一颗消失的弹丸，倏地一闪，便没了踪影。

===Original===
All but one leg! Dr. Silence just had the time and the presence of mind to seize upon the left ankle and boot as it disappeared, and to this he held on for several seconds like grim death. Yet all the time he knew it was a foolish and useless thing to do.

The foot was in his grasp one moment, and the next it seemed—this was the only way he could describe it—inside his own skin and bones, and at the same time outside his hand and all round it. It seemed mixed up in some amazing way with his own flesh and blood. Then it was gone, and he was tightly grasping a draught of heated air.

"Gone! gone! gone!" cried a thick, whispering voice, somewhere deep within his own consciousness. "Lost! lost! lost!" it repeated, growing fainter and fainter till at length it vanished into nothing and the last signs of Mr. Racine Mudge vanished with it.

===Chinese===
只除了一条腿！寂静医生恰好来得及、也恰好有这分镇定，在那左脚踝和靴子消失之际一把攥住，他死死地抓住不放，足有数秒之久。可他心里始终明白，这么做既愚蠢又无用。

那只脚前一瞬还在他掌中，下一瞬便仿佛——他只能这样来形容——钻进了他自己的皮肉骨血之中，同时又在他手之外、把手整个儿围住。它仿佛以某种惊人的方式与他自己的血肉搅在了一起。接着它便没了，他紧紧攥住的，不过是一团热气。

"去了！去了！去了！"一个浑浊的低语声，在他自己意识的深处某处叫道。"失了！失了！失了！"它一遍遍重复，越来越轻，越来越轻，到末了终于归于虚无，而雷辛·马奇先生最后的痕迹，也随之一同消失了。

===Original===
John Silence locked his red book and replaced it in the cabinet, which he fastened with a click, and when Barker answered the bell he inquired if Mr. Mudge had left a card upon the table. It appeared that he had, and when the servant returned with it, Dr. Silence read the address and made a note of it. It was in North London.

"Mr. Mudge has gone," he said quietly to Barker, noticing his expression of alarm.

"He's not taken his 'at with him, sir."

"Mr. Mudge requires no hat where he is now," continued the doctor, stooping to poke the fire. "But he may return for it—"

"And the humbrella, sir."

"And the umbrella."

===Chinese===
约翰·寂静锁上他那本红书，放回柜中，"咔哒"一声锁好。待巴克应铃而来，他问起马奇先生可曾把名片留在桌上。据说留了，仆人取来时，寂静医生读了上面的地址，记了下来。地址在北伦敦。

"马奇先生走了。"他注意到巴克那惊惶的神色，平静地对他说。

"他没把帽子带走，先生。"

"马奇先生如今所在之处，用不着帽子。"医生接着说，俯身拨了拨炉火。"不过他也许会回来取——"

"还有那把伞，先生。"

"还有那把伞。"

===Original===
"He didn't go out *my* way, sir, if you please," stuttered the amazed servant, his curiosity overcoming his nervousness.

"Mr. Mudge has his own way of coming and going, and prefers it. If he returns by the door at any time remember to bring him instantly to me, and be kind and gentle with him and ask no questions. Also, remember, Barker, to think pleasantly, sympathetically, affectionately of him while he is away. Mr. Mudge is a very suffering gentleman."

Barker bowed and went out of the room backwards, gasping and feeling round the inside of his collar with three very hot fingers of one hand.

===Chinese===
"他可不是从*我*这边出去的，先生，恕我这么讲。"那惊愕的仆人结结巴巴地说，他的好奇压过了紧张。

"马奇先生有他自己来去的法子，他也乐意如此。他若是何时从门里回来，记着立刻领他来见我，要和善、温存地待他，什么也莫问。还有，记着，巴克，他不在的时候，要想着他的好，要同情他，要带着关爱去想他。马奇先生是个极受苦的先生。"

巴克一躬身，倒退着出了屋子，喘着气，一只手的三根滚烫手指在衣领里面摸了一圈。

===Original===
It was two days later when he brought in a telegram to the study. Dr. Silence opened it, and read as follows:

"Bombay. Just slipped out again. All safe. Have blocked entrances. Thousand thanks. Address Cooks, London.—Mudge."

Dr. Silence looked up and saw Barker staring at him bewilderingly. It occurred to him that somehow he knew the contents of the telegram.

"Make a parcel of Mr. Mudge's things," he said briefly, "and address them Thomas Cook & Sons, Ludgate Circus. And send them there exactly a month from today and marked 'To be called for.' "

"Yes, sir," said Barker, leaving the room with a deep sigh and a hurried glance at the wastepaper basket where his master had dropped the pink paper.

===Chinese===
两天之后，他送了一份电报进书房。寂静医生拆开一看，上头写着：

"孟买。又滑了出来。一切平安。已堵上各入口。万分感谢。地址库克公司，伦敦。——马奇"

寂静医生抬起头，看见巴克正一脸茫然地盯着他。他忽然想到，这仆人竟似已知道了电报的内容。

"把马奇先生的东西打成包裹，"他简短地说，"地址写托马斯·库克父子公司，拉德门马戏广场。整一个月后寄到那里，注明'留交自取'。"

"是，先生。"巴克说，深叹一声出了屋子，临走匆匆瞥了一眼废纸篓——主人已把那张粉色的电报纸丢在了里头。

## Case II / 案例 II

## Ancient Sorceries / 古老的巫术

### I / I

===Original===
There are, it would appear, certain wholly unremarkable persons, with none of the characteristics that invite adventure, who yet once or twice in the course of their smooth lives undergo an experience so strange that the world catches its breath﻿—and looks the other way! And it was cases of this kind, perhaps, more than any other, that fell into the widespread net of John Silence, the psychic doctor, and, appealing to his deep humanity, to his patience, and to his great qualities of spiritual sympathy, led often to the revelation of problems of the strangest complexity, and of the profoundest possible human interest.

Matters that seemed almost too curious and fantastic for belief he loved to trace to their hidden sources. To unravel a tangle in the very soul of things﻿—and to release a suffering human soul in the process﻿—was with him a veritable passion. And the knots he untied were, indeed, after passing strange.

===Chinese===
世间似乎确有一些全然不起眼的人，身上没有半点招惹奇遇的品性，却在其顺遂的一生中，有那么一两次，经历了一桩如此离奇的事情，令世人不由得倒吸一口凉气——随即掉过头去，假装不曾看见！而恰恰是这一类的案子，或许比任何别的案子都更多落入了约翰·寂静医生——那位灵异医生——撒开的大网之中；它们触动了他深沉的人性之心，触动了他的耐心，触动了他那伟大的心灵同情之力，往往由此揭示出一些其复杂之奇异、其旨趣之深远都到了无以复加地步的难题。

那些看似离奇到近乎不可置信的事，他偏喜爱追根溯源，寻到它们隐秘的源头。在万物的灵魂深处理清一团乱麻——并在这一过程中释放一颗受苦的人类灵魂——于他实为一种名副其实的痴迷。而他解开的那些结，确也当真离奇到了极点。

===Original===
The world, of course, asks for some plausible basis to which it can attach credence﻿—something it can, at least, pretend to explain. The adventurous type it can understand: such people carry about with them an adequate explanation of their exciting lives, and their characters obviously drive them into the circumstances which produce the adventures. It expects nothing else from them, and is satisfied. But dull, ordinary folk have no right to out-of-the-way experiences, and the world having been led to expect otherwise, is disappointed with them, not to say shocked. Its complacent judgment has been rudely disturbed.

"Such a thing happened to *that* man!" it cries﻿—"a commonplace person like that! It is too absurd! There must be something wrong!"

===Chinese===
世人，当然，总要求某个说得过去的基础来附上它的轻信——至少要求某个它能装模作样加以解释的东西。冒险型的那一类人它是懂得的：这类人身上自带一套对其刺激人生的充分解释，他们的性情明摆着会驱使他们走进那些催生奇遇的境况。世人对他们别无所求，心满意足。可 dull 而平庸的小人物，却没有资格遭遇什么离奇之事；世人既已被引导着做了别的指望，便对他们大失所望，甚至不无震惊。它那自满的判词被粗暴地搅乱了。

"这样的事竟然发生在*那个*人身上！"它嚷道——"那样一个平庸的人！太荒唐了！一定哪里出了岔子！"

===Original===
Yet there could be no question that something did actually happen to little Arthur Vezin, something of the curious nature he described to Dr. Silence. Outwardly or inwardly, it happened beyond a doubt, and in spite of the jeers of his few friends who heard the tale, and observed wisely that "such a thing might perhaps have come to Iszard, that crackbrained Iszard, or to that odd fish Minski, but it could never have happened to commonplace little Vezin, who was foreordained to live and die according to scale."

But, whatever his method of death was, Vezin certainly did not "live according to scale" so far as this particular event in his otherwise uneventful life was concerned; and to hear him recount it, and watch his pale delicate features change, and hear his voice grow softer and more hushed as he proceeded, was to know the conviction that his halting words perhaps failed sometimes to convey. He lived the thing over again each time he told it. His whole personality became muffled in the recital. It subdued him more than ever, so that the tale became a lengthy apology for an experience that he deprecated. He appeared to excuse himself and ask your pardon for having dared to take part in so fantastic an episode. For little Vezin was a timid, gentle, sensitive soul, rarely able to assert himself, tender to man and beast, and almost constitutionally unable to say No, or to claim many things that should rightly have been his. His whole scheme of life seemed utterly remote from anything more exciting than missing a train or losing an umbrella on an omnibus. And when this curious event came upon him he was already more years beyond forty than his friends suspected or he cared to admit.

===Chinese===
然而，小个子亚瑟·维赞身上确确实实发生了某件事，那是毋庸置疑的——一桩具有他所描述的那种离奇性质的事，他对寂静医生一五一十地讲了。无论在外还是在内，它确凿无疑地发生了；任凭他那几个听了这故事的朋友如何嗤笑，任凭他们怎样自作聪明地评论说"这样的事嘛，也许会落到伊扎德头上，那个神经兮兮的伊扎德，或者是明斯基那个怪人头上，但绝不可能发生在平庸的小维赞身上——他命中注定要按部就班地活着、死去"——也无济于事。

可是，无论他的死法如何，至少就他那原本波澜不惊的一生中这桩特殊事件而言，维赞确实没有"按部就班地活过"；而听他讲述此事，看着他苍白而精致的面容起了变化，听着他的声音越说越轻、越说越低沉，你便能感受到一种他的磕磕绊绊的话语有时或许未能传达的信念。他每讲一次，便将那件事重新活过一遍。他整个人仿佛被裹进了讲述之中。它比任何时候都更深地压制着他，以至于这故事成了一篇冗长的致歉，为他所不敢认的一番经历赔罪。他仿佛在为自己开脱，在请求你的原谅——原谅他竟敢参与了如此离奇的一段遭遇。因为小维赞是个胆怯、温柔、敏感的灵魂，极少能够自作主张，对人待畜都一腔柔软，又仿佛天生就不会说"不"，也不会去争取许多本该属于他的东西。他整个的人生图景，似乎与任何比"错过一趟火车"或"在公共马车上丢了一把伞"更刺激的事情都了无瓜葛。而当这桩古怪的事件降临时，他已年过四十——不止四十的年头，比他的朋友们所以为的、比他自己肯承认的还要多。

===Original===
John Silence, who heard him speak of his experience more than once, said that he sometimes left out certain details and put in others; yet they were all obviously true. The whole scene was unforgettably cinematographed on to his mind. None of the details were imagined or invented. And when he told the story with them all complete, the effect was undeniable. His appealing brown eyes shone, and much of the charming personality, usually so carefully repressed, came forward and revealed itself. His modesty was always there, of course, but in the telling he forgot the present and allowed himself to appear almost vividly as he lived again in the past of his adventure.

===Chinese===
约翰·寂静不止一次听他讲起这段经历，他说维赞有时略去某些细节，有时又添上别的；然而那些细节显然都千真万确。整幕场景已不可磨灭地印在了他的脑海里，如同一部影片。没有一个细节是想象或编造出来的。而当他带着所有细节完整地讲述这个故事时，那效果是不容置疑的。他那讨人喜欢的棕色眼睛闪着光，那通常被小心翼翼压抑着的迷人性情，便纷纷涌上前来自行显露。他的谦逊自然始终都在，可在讲述中他忘了当下，任自己几乎鲜活地呈现出来——他在冒险的往昔中重新活过一遍。

===Original===
He was on the way home when it happened, crossing northern France from some mountain trip or other where he buried himself solitary-wise every summer. He had nothing but an unregistered bag in the rack, and the train was jammed to suffocation, most of the passengers being unredeemed holiday English. He disliked them, not because they were his fellow-countrymen, but because they were noisy and obtrusive, obliterating with their big limbs and tweed clothing all the quieter tints of the day that brought him satisfaction and enabled him to melt into insignificance and forget that he was anybody. These English clashed about him like a brass band, making him feel vaguely that he ought to be more self-assertive and obstreperous, and that he did not claim insistently enough all kinds of things that he didn't want and that were really valueless, such as corner seats, windows up or down, and so forth.

So that he felt uncomfortable in the train, and wished the journey were over and he was back again living with his unmarried sister in Surbiton.

===Chinese===
事情发生时，他正在回家的路上，穿过法国北部——他每年夏天都要独自隐到某座山里去消磨一番。行李架上只有他一只未登记的旅行袋，火车挤得令人窒息，乘客大多是清一色的英国度假客。他不喜欢他们，倒不是因为他们是他的同胞，而是因为他们吵吵嚷嚷、招摇扎眼，用他们粗大的四肢和花呢衣裳抹去了白日里那些更安静的色彩——正是那些色彩带给他满足，使他得以融化到微不足道中去、忘记自己还是个什么人物。这些英国人在他身边叮叮当当地碰撞，像一支铜管乐队，叫他在一种模糊的感觉中觉得自己本该更张扬、更喧闹一些，觉得自己还不够执着地去索取种种他根本不想要、其实也毫无价值的东西，比如角落的座位、窗户该开还是该关，等等诸如此类。

于是他在火车上觉得很不自在，巴不得这趟旅程赶紧结束，好回到瑟比顿，与他那未出嫁的姐姐同住。

===Original===
And when the train stopped for ten panting minutes at the little station in northern France, and he got out to stretch his legs on the platform, and saw to his dismay a further batch of the British Isles debouching from another train, it suddenly seemed impossible to him to continue the journey. Even *his* flabby soul revolted, and the idea of staying a night in the little town and going on next day by a slower, emptier train, flashed into his mind. The guard was already shouting "*en voiture*" and the corridor of his compartment was already packed when the thought came to him. And, for once, he acted with decision and rushed to snatch his bag.

Finding the corridor and steps impassable, he tapped at the window (for he had a corner seat) and begged the Frenchman who sat opposite to hand his luggage out to him, explaining in his wretched French that he intended to break the journey there. And this elderly Frenchman, he declared, gave him a look, half of warning, half of reproach, that to his dying day he could never forget; handed the bag through the window of the moving train; and at the same time poured into his ears a long sentence, spoken rapidly and low, of which he was able to comprehend only the last few words: "*À cause du sommeil et à cause des chats.*"

===Chinese===
而当火车在法国北部那座小站喘着粗气停了十分钟、他下车到站台上活动活动腿脚，又惊恐地看到又一批不列颠群岛的子民正从另一列火车里鱼贯而出时，继续这趟旅程忽然变得不可能了。连*他*那软塌塌的灵魂都起了反叛之念，在这座小城留宿一夜、第二天再搭一趟更慢更空的火车走的念头，一闪便掠过他的脑海。列车员已经在喊"*en voiture*"了，他那节车厢的过道已经挤得水泄不通——恰在此时，这个念头来了。而这一次，他竟果断地行动起来，冲回去抓自己的旅行袋。

过道和车门口已挤得寸步难行，他便敲了敲车窗（他坐在角落的位子），请坐在对面的那个法国人帮他把行李递出来，用他糟糕的法语解释说自己打算在此地中途下车。而这个上了年纪的法国人，他后来坚称，给了他一个眼神——半是警告，半是责备——那眼神他到死的那一天都无法忘怀；那人把旅行袋从正在移动的车窗里递了出来，同时朝他耳边倒了一长串话，说得又急又低，他只听懂了最后几个词："*À cause du sommeil et à cause des chats.*"

===Original===
In reply to Dr. Silence, whose singular psychic acuteness at once seized upon this Frenchman as a vital point in the adventure, Vezin admitted that the man had impressed him favourably from the beginning, though without being able to explain why. They had sat facing one another during the four hours of the journey, and though no conversation had passed between them﻿—Vezin was timid about his stuttering French﻿—he confessed that his eyes were being continually drawn to his face, almost, he felt, to rudeness, and that each, by a dozen nameless little politenesses and attentions, had evinced the desire to be kind. The men liked each other and their personalities did not clash, or would not have clashed had they chanced to come to terms of acquaintance. The Frenchman, indeed, seemed to have exercised a silent protective influence over the insignificant little Englishman, and without words or gestures betrayed that he wished him well and would gladly have been of service to him.

"And this sentence that he hurled at you after the bag?" asked John Silence, smiling that peculiarly sympathetic smile that always melted the prejudices of his patient, "were you unable to follow it exactly?"

===Chinese===
寂静医生那异乎寻常的通灵敏锐，当即把这个法国人锁定为整桩冒险中的一个关键所在；面对医生的追问，维赞承认那人从一开始就给了他好感，尽管他说不上来为什么。旅途的四个小时里他们面对面坐着，虽然彼此间不曾有过交谈——维赞对自己结结巴巴的法语心存怯意——但他承认，自己的目光总被不由自主地引向对方的脸，几乎到了失礼的地步；他还说，他们各自都通过一打叫不出名来的小小殷勤与体贴，流露了愿与对方亲善的心意。两人彼此喜欢，性情并不相冲——或者倘若碰巧结下了交情，也不会相冲。那法国人似乎确实对这个不起眼的英国小个子施展了一种无声的保护之力，不借言语、不凭手势便表露出他心怀善意，乐意为他效劳。

"那么，递完旅行袋之后他朝你甩过来的那句话呢？"约翰·寂静问道，嘴角浮起那特有的、总能融化病人戒备的同情微笑，"你就一点也跟不上它的意思？"

===Original===
"It was so quick and low and vehement," explained Vezin, in his small voice, "that I missed practically the whole of it. I only caught the few words at the very end, because he spoke them so clearly, and his face was bent down out of the carriage window so near to mine."

" ' *À cause du sommeil et à cause des chats'?*" repeated Dr. Silence, as though half speaking to himself.

"That's it exactly," said Vezin; "which, I take it, means something like 'because of sleep and because of the cats,' doesn't it?"

"Certainly, that's how I should translate it," the doctor observed shortly, evidently not wishing to interrupt more than necessary.

"And the rest of the sentence﻿—all the first part I couldn't understand, I mean﻿—was a warning not to do something﻿—not to stop in the town, or at some particular place in the town, perhaps. That was the impression it made on me."

Then, of course, the train rushed off, and left Vezin standing on the platform alone and rather forlorn.

===Chinese===
"那话说得太快、太低、太急了，"维赞用他细细的嗓音解释道，"我几乎整句都没听清。我只抓住了最后几个词，因为他说那几个词的时候说得格外清晰，而他的脸从车窗里探下来，离我的脸近得不能再近。"

"'*À cause du sommeil et à cause des chats?*'？"寂静医生重复了一遍，像是半在自言自语。

"一字不差，"维赞说；"我猜这话的意思大概是'因为睡眠，也因为那些猫'之类，是不是？"

"没错，我也会这么译。"医生简短地说了一句，显然不愿多做打断。

"而那句话的其余部分——我是说我听不懂的全部前半段——是一句警告，叫我不要做某件事——不要在这城里停留，也许是在城里某个特定的地方停留。这是它留给我的印象。"

随后，自然是，火车轰隆隆地开走了，把维赞一个人丢在站台上，孤零零的，颇为凄凉。

===Original===
The little town climbed in straggling fashion up a sharp hill rising out of the plain at the back of the station, and was crowned by the twin towers of the ruined cathedral peeping over the summit. From the station itself it looked uninteresting and modern, but the fact was that the medieval position lay out of sight just beyond the crest. And once he reached the top and entered the old streets, he stepped clean out of modern life into a bygone century. The noise and bustle of the crowded train seemed days away. The spirit of this silent hill-town, remote from tourists and motorcars, dreaming its own quiet life under the autumn sun, rose up and cast its spell upon him. Long before he recognised this spell he acted under it. He walked softly, almost on tiptoe, down the winding narrow streets where the gables all but met over his head, and he entered the doorway of the solitary inn with a deprecating and modest demeanour that was in itself an apology for intruding upon the place and disturbing its dream.

At first, however, Vezin said, he noticed very little of all this. The attempt at analysis came much later. What struck him then was only the delightful contrast of the silence and peace after the dust and noisy rattle of the train. He felt soothed and stroked like a cat.

===Chinese===
那座小城以一种散漫的姿态攀上一座陡峭的山丘，山丘从车站背后的平原上拔起，山顶上探出那座倾圮的大教堂的孪生钟楼，如同一顶冠冕。从车站这边望去，它毫不起眼，一派现代面目，可实情是，中世纪的旧城就藏在山脊那边、视线之外。而一旦他到达山顶、走进那些老街，他便一步跨出了现代生活，踏进了往昔的某一个世纪。拥挤火车上的喧闹与忙乱，似乎已远在几天之前。这座寂静的山城的灵魂——远离游客与汽车，在秋日的阳光下做着自己安静的梦——升起来，将它的咒符笼罩在了他身上。远在他辨认出这道咒符之前，他便已受其驱使了。他轻轻地走，几乎踮着脚尖，沿着蜿蜒的窄街前行，街道两旁的山墙几乎在他头顶上方相接；他走进那家唯一的客栈的门洞，带着一种谦卑而自抑的神态——那神态本身就是一声致歉，为闯入此地、惊扰了它的清梦而赔罪。

然而，维赞说，起初他对这一切几乎浑然不觉。分析的尝试是许久之后才有的。当时打动他的，不过是火车的尘土与嘈杂的哐当声之后，那份寂静与安宁所带来的悦目的反差。他觉得被抚慰了，被轻轻抚摸了，像一只猫。

===Original===
"Like a cat, you said?" interrupted John Silence, quickly catching him up.

"Yes. At the very start I felt that." He laughed apologetically. "I felt as though the warmth and the stillness and the comfort made me purr. It seemed to be the general mood of the whole place﻿—then."

===Chinese===
"你说，像一只猫？"约翰·寂静插话，一把抓住了他。

"是的。一开始我就有这种感觉。"他带着歉意笑了笑。"我觉得好像那份温暖、那份静谧和那份舒坦让我打起了呼噜。这似乎是整个地方那时的一般气息。"

===Original===
The inn, a rambling ancient house, the atmosphere of the old coaching days still about it, apparently did not welcome him too warmly. He felt he was only tolerated, he said. But it was cheap and comfortable, and the delicious cup of afternoon tea he ordered at once made him feel really very pleased with himself for leaving the train in this bold, original way. For to him it had seemed bold and original. He felt something of a dog. His room, too, soothed him with its dark panelling and low irregular ceiling, and the long sloping passage that led to it seemed the natural pathway to a real Chamber of Sleep﻿—a little dim cubby hole out of the world where noise could not enter. It looked upon the courtyard at the back. It was all very charming, and made him think of himself as dressed in very soft velvet somehow, and the floors seemed padded, the walls provided with cushions. The sounds of the streets could not penetrate there. It was an atmosphere of absolute rest that surrounded him.

===Chinese===
那家客栈，一幢散漫铺排的老房子，旧时驿马车时代的气息犹存，显然并不十分热情地欢迎他。他说，他觉得自己不过是被勉强容忍罢了。但这里既便宜又舒适，他当即点的那一杯美味的午后红茶，令他真心觉得自己这般大胆、这般别出心裁地下了火车，实在是一桩得意之举。因为在他看来，这已算是大胆而别出心裁了。他觉得自己多少有了点气概。他的房间也令他心神安宁——暗色的护墙板、低矮而不规则的天花板，通往此屋的那条长长的、带着坡度的走廊，仿佛是通往一间真正的安眠之室的自然路径——一个远离尘世、昏暗的小小洞穴，喧嚣不得而入。房间朝向后面的庭院。一切都如此迷人，使他不由得想象自己仿佛穿了一身极柔软的天鹅绒，地板仿佛垫了衬，墙壁仿佛装了靠垫。街上的声响穿不进来。环绕他的是一种绝对的安息。

===Original===
On engaging the two-franc room he had interviewed the only person who seemed to be about that sleepy afternoon, an elderly waiter with Dundreary whiskers and a drowsy courtesy, who had ambled lazily towards him across the stone yard; but on coming downstairs again for a little promenade in the town before dinner he encountered the proprietress herself. She was a large woman whose hands, feet, and features seemed to swim towards him out of a sea of person. They emerged, so to speak. But she had great dark, vivacious eyes that counteracted the bulk of her body, and betrayed the fact that in reality she was both vigorous and alert. When he first caught sight of her she was knitting in a low chair against the sunlight of the wall, and something at once made him see her as a great tabby cat, dozing, yet awake, heavily sleepy, and yet at the same time prepared for instantaneous action. A great mouser on the watch occurred to him.

She took him in with a single comprehensive glance that was polite without being cordial. Her neck, he noticed, was extraordinarily supple in spite of its proportions, for it turned so easily to follow him, and the head it carried bowed so very flexibly.

===Chinese===
订下那间两个法郎的房间时，他见过这天午后昏昏欲睡的时分里唯一似乎还在的人——一个上了年纪的侍者，蓄着邓德里式络腮胡，带着一种昏昏欲睡的客气，懒洋洋地穿过石板铺的院子踱到他跟前；但等到他晚饭前再次下楼、想到城里随便走走时，却碰上了老板娘本人。她是个大块头的女人，手、脚和五官仿佛是从一片人的海洋里朝他游过来的。它们可以说是浮现出来的。但她有一双又大又黑、活泼灵动的眼睛，抵消了她身躯的庞大，泄露了她实则精力充沛、警觉机敏的底细。他第一眼看见她时，她正坐在墙边阳光下一张矮椅子里织毛线，某种东西顿时让他把她看作一只硕大的虎斑猫，似睡非睡，沉沉欲眠，却又随时可以一跃而起。他脑海里浮现出一只伺机而动的大捕鼠猫的形象。

她用一道全面的、一扫而过的目光将他收入眼中，客气而不热络。他注意到，她的脖子尽管粗壮，却异常柔韧，因为它转动起来毫不费力地追随着他，它顶着的那颗脑袋弯得十分灵活。

===Original===
"But when she looked at me, you know," said Vezin, with that little apologetic smile in his brown eyes, and that faintly deprecating gesture of the shoulders that was characteristic of him, "the odd notion came to me that really she had intended to make quite a different movement, and that with a single bound she could have leaped at me across the width of that stone yard and pounced upon me like some huge cat upon a mouse."

He laughed a little soft laugh, and Dr. Silence made a note in his book without interrupting, while Vezin proceeded in a tone as though he feared he had already told too much and more than we could believe.

"Very soft, yet very active she was, for all her size and mass, and I felt she knew what I was doing even after I had passed and was behind her back. She spoke to me, and her voice was smooth and running. She asked if I had my luggage, and was comfortable in my room, and then added that dinner was at seven o'clock, and that they were very early people in this little country town. Clearly, she intended to convey that late hours were not encouraged."

===Chinese===
"可她看向我的时候，你知道，"维赞说，棕色的眼睛里带着那丝歉意微微的笑，两肩做出那微微自抑的手势——那是他的招牌动作——"我心里忽然冒出一个古怪的念头：其实她原本打算做一个全然不同的动作，只消一纵身，她就可以越过那整个石板院子的宽度朝我扑来，像一只巨猫扑向一只老鼠。"

他轻轻地笑了一声。寂静医生在本子上记了一笔，没有打断，维赞则继续往下说，语气像是在担心自己已经说得太过了，说得太多了，超出了我们所能相信的。

"她是极柔软的，却又极其敏捷，尽管有那般体量和块头；我觉得，哪怕我已经走过去、到了她背后，她也知道我在做什么。她跟我说话，声音光滑而流淌。她问我的行李是否到了、房间里住着可还舒适，然后又说晚餐是七点，还说这座乡下小城里的人都是起得很早的。显然，她的意思是不提倡晚睡晚起。"

===Original===
Evidently, she contrived by voice and manner to give him the impression that here he would be "managed," that everything would be arranged and planned for him, and that he had nothing to do but fall into the groove and obey. No decided action or sharp personal effort would be looked for from him. It was the very reverse of the train. He walked quietly out into the street feeling soothed and peaceful. He realised that he was in a milieu that suited him and stroked him the right way. It was so much easier to be obedient. He began to purr again, and to feel that all the town purred with him.

About the streets of that little town he meandered gently, falling deeper and deeper into the spirit of repose that characterised it. With no special aim he wandered up and down, and to and fro. The September sunshine fell slantingly over the roofs. Down winding alleyways, fringed with tumbling gables and open casements, he caught fairylike glimpses of the great plain below, and of the meadows and yellow copses lying like a dream map in the haze. The spell of the past held very potently here, he felt.

===Chinese===
显然，她设法以声音和举止给他这样一种印象：在这里，他会被"安排"好的，一切都会替他打点妥当、筹划周全，他什么也不用做，只需顺其道而行、乖乖听命便是。别指望他会做出什么果断的行动或有力的个人努力。这与火车上恰好截然相反。他安安静静地走到街上，觉得身心舒坦、一片宁和。他意识到自己置身于一个与他相宜的、顺毛抚他的环境之中。服从是多么容易的事啊。他又开始打起呼噜来，觉得整座城都在和他一同打呼噜。

他在这座小城的街巷间轻轻地信步漫游，越走越深地沉入那弥漫全城的安谧气息之中。他上上下下、来来回回，没有特定的目的。九月的阳光斜斜地洒过屋顶。沿着蜿蜒的小巷——两旁歪斜的山墙和敞开的窗户是它们的流苏——他不时瞥见下方那片大平原童话般的远景，以及草场和黄色的小树林，它们在薄霭中如同一张梦中的地图。往昔的咒符在这里笼罩得格外有力，他感到。

===Original===
The streets were full of picturesquely garbed men and women, all busy enough, going their respective ways; but no one took any notice of him or turned to stare at his obviously English appearance. He was even able to forget that with his tourist appearance he was a false note in a charming picture, and he melted more and more into the scene, feeling delightfully insignificant and unimportant and unselfconscious. It was like becoming part of a softly coloured dream which he did not even realise to be a dream.

On the eastern side the hill fell away more sharply, and the plain below ran off rather suddenly into a sea of gathering shadows in which the little patches of woodland looked like islands and the stubble fields like deep water. Here he strolled along the old ramparts of ancient fortifications that once had been formidable, but now were only vision-like with their charming mingling of broken grey walls and wayward vine and ivy. From the broad coping on which he sat for a moment, level with the rounded tops of clipped plane trees, he saw the esplanade far below lying in shadow. Here and there a yellow sunbeam crept in and lay upon the fallen yellow leaves, and from the height he looked down and saw that the townsfolk were walking to and fro in the cool of the evening. He could just hear the sound of their slow footfalls, and the murmur of their voices floated up to him through the gaps between the trees. The figures looked like shadows as he caught glimpses of their quiet movements far below.

===Chinese===
街上满是衣着鲜亮、颇有古意的男男女女，都够忙的，各走各的路；可没有谁留意他，也没有谁回过头来盯住他那副一看便知是英国人的模样。他甚至能够忘记：以他那副游客的打扮，他是这幅迷人画卷中一个不和谐的假音符；他越来越深地融进这幅景象之中，美妙地感到自己无足轻重、无关紧要、忘掉了自我。这就像成为一个色彩柔和的梦的一部分——而他甚至没有意识到那是一个梦。

东面那侧山势陡然下降，下方的平原相当突然地遁入一片渐聚的暗影之海，其间小块的林地如同岛屿，收割后的残茬田如同深水。他在古要塞的旧城墙上散步，这些城墙昔年曾令人生畏，如今却只如幻影一般——残破的灰色墙体与恣意的藤蔓和常春藤迷人地交织在一起。他在宽阔的墙头上坐了片刻，头顶与修剪过的梧桐那浑圆的树冠齐平，望见下方远远的散步道卧在阴影中。这儿那儿，一道黄色的日光偷偷钻进来，落在枯黄的落叶上；从高处俯瞰，他看到城中居民在傍晚的清凉中来来往往。他勉强能听到他们缓慢的脚步声，人语的低喃穿过树木间的间隙飘上来。那些远远的、悄然移动的身影，在他看来如同影子。

===Original===
He sat there for some time pondering, bathed in the waves of murmurs and half-lost echoes that rose to his ears, muffled by the leaves of the plane trees. The whole town, and the little hill out of which it grew as naturally as an ancient wood, seemed to him like a being lying there half asleep on the plain and crooning to itself as it dozed.

And, presently, as he sat lazily melting into its dream, a sound of horns and strings and wood instruments rose to his ears, and the town band began to play at the far end of the crowded terrace below to the accompaniment of a very soft, deep-throated drum. Vezin was very sensitive to music, knew about it intelligently, and had even ventured, unknown to his friends, upon the composition of quiet melodies with low-running chords which he played to himself with the soft pedal when no one was about. And this music floating up through the trees from an invisible and doubtless very picturesque band of the townspeople wholly charmed him. He recognised nothing that they played, and it sounded as though they were simply improvising without a conductor. No definitely marked time ran through the pieces, which ended and began oddly after the fashion of wind through an Aeolian harp. It was part of the place and scene, just as the dying sunlight and faintly breathing wind were part of the scene and hour, and the mellow notes of old-fashioned plaintive horns, pierced here and there by the sharper strings, all half smothered by the continuous booming of the deep drum, touched his soul with a curiously potent spell that was almost too engrossing to be quite pleasant.

===Chinese===
他在那里坐了些时候，沉思着，沉浸在那些飘上耳畔的低语和半已消散的回声的波浪里，梧桐的叶子将它们捂得闷闷的。整座城，连同它从中长出的那座小山——那山生出了城，一如一片古林从地里长出那般自然——在他看来就像一个生灵，半睡半醒地卧在平原上，一边打盹一边哼着低低的调子。

不久，当他慵懒地坐在那里、融化进它的梦中时，一阵铜管、弦乐和木管乐器的声响飘上耳畔，城里的乐队在下方那熙攘的露台远端开始演奏了，伴着一只极轻柔的、嗓音深沉的鼓。维赞对音乐极为敏感，颇有见地，甚至瞒着朋友们偷偷尝试过创作一些安静的旋律和低回的和弦，趁四下无人时用弱音踏板弹给自己听。而这段从树丛间飘上来的音乐——来自一支看不见的、想必十分鲜亮的市民乐队——将他完全迷住了。他们奏的曲子他一首也辨认不出，听起来仿佛他们不过是在没有指挥的情形下即兴发挥。曲子里没有明确标定的节拍，起止的方式都很古怪，如同穿过一具风神琴的清风。它是此地此景的一部分，正如将逝的日光和若有若无的风也是此景此时的一部分；那古式哀婉的铜号的醇厚音符——这里那里被更尖锐的弦乐刺穿——全部被那深沉的鼓持续的隆隆声捂住了一半，以一种奇异而有力的咒符触动了他的灵魂，那咒符的磁力之强，几乎到了令人沉溺而不甚舒服的地步。

===Original===
There was a certain queer sense of bewitchment in it all. The music seemed to him oddly unartificial. It made him think of trees swept by the wind, of night breezes singing among wires and chimney-stacks, or in the rigging of invisible ships; or﻿—and the simile leaped up in his thoughts with a sudden sharpness of suggestion﻿—a chorus of animals, of wild creatures, somewhere in desolate places of the world, crying and singing as animals will, to the moon. He could fancy he heard the wailing, half-human cries of cats upon the tiles at night, rising and falling with weird intervals of sound, and this music, muffled by distance and the trees, made him think of a queer company of these creatures on some roof far away in the sky, uttering their solemn music to one another and the moon in chorus.

It was, he felt at the time, a singular image to occur to him, yet it expressed his sensation pictorially better than anything else. The instruments played such impossibly odd intervals, and the crescendos and diminuendos were so very suggestive of cat-land on the tiles at night, rising swiftly, dropping without warning to deep notes again, and all in such strange confusion of discords and accords. But, at the same time a plaintive sweetness resulted on the whole, and the discords of these half-broken instruments were so singular that they did not distress his musical soul like fiddles out of tune.

He listened a long time, wholly surrendering himself as his character was, and then strolled homewards in the dusk as the air grew chilly.

===Chinese===
这一切之中，有一种古怪的、被施了蛊的感觉。那音乐在他听来有一种奇异的非人造之感。它令他想起被风掠过的树林，夜风在电线和烟囱之间歌唱，或在看不见的船的索具间歌唱；又或者——这个比喻在他脑海中以一种突如其来的、尖锐的暗示力跳了出来——一群动物的合唱，一群野兽的合唱，在世间某个荒凉的地方，像动物那样对着月亮又叫又唱。他恍惚觉得自己听到了夜间瓦屋顶上猫的哀号，那半像人声的叫声，以怪异的间隔起伏升降；而这段被距离和树丛捂住了的音乐，让他想到这样一群生灵聚在一处遥远天际的屋顶上，对着彼此、对着月亮齐声唱出它们庄严的曲调。

他觉得在当时，这是一个会浮上心头的奇异意象，然而它比任何别的说法都更生动地表达了他的感受。那些乐器奏出了一些不可能的、古怪的音程，渐强和渐弱如此逼真地令人联想到夜间瓦屋顶上的猫之国——猛地升上去，又不经警告骤然跌回低音——一切都在不协和与协和的奇异混乱之中。但与此同时，整体上却生出一种哀婉的甜美，而那些半破损的乐器所奏出的不协和是如此奇异，竟不像走调的小提琴那样折磨他的乐感。

他听了许久，一任自己的性情全然地交付出去；而后，趁空气凉了下来，在暮色中踱了回去。

===Original===
"There was nothing to alarm?" put in Dr. Silence briefly.

"Absolutely nothing," said Vezin; "but you know it was all so fantastical and charming that my imagination was profoundly impressed. Perhaps, too," he continued, gently explanatory, "it was this stirring of my imagination that caused other impressions; for, as I walked back, the spell of the place began to steal over me in a dozen ways, though all intelligible ways. But there were other things I could not account for in the least, even then."

"Incidents, you mean?"

"Hardly incidents, I think. A lot of vivid sensations crowded themselves upon my mind and I could trace them to no causes. It was just after sunset and the tumbled old buildings traced magical outlines against an opalescent sky of gold and red. The dusk was running down the twisted streets. All round the hill the plain pressed in like a dim sea, its level rising with the darkness. The spell of this kind of scene, you know, can be very moving, and it was so that night. Yet I felt that what came to me had nothing directly to do with the mystery and wonder of the scene."

===Chinese===
"没有什么让你警觉的？"寂静医生简短地插了一句。

"完全没有，"维赞说；"可你知道，那一切是如此奇幻而迷人，我的想象力被深深打动了。也许，"他带着温和的解释语气继续说，"正是想象力的这番涌动引出了其他的印象；因为在我走回去的路上，此地的咒符开始以一打方式悄悄爬上我的心头——尽管都是说得通的方式。但还有些别的，我即便在当时也丝毫解释不了。"

"你是说，一些事件？"

"算不上事件，我想。许许多多鲜明的感受涌进了我的脑海，我却追溯不到任何因由。那是在日落后不久，参差的老建筑在金红两色的蛋白石天空映衬下勾出魔法般的轮廓。暮色顺着扭曲的街道流淌下来。山丘四周，平原像一片幽暗的海挤压而来，海平面随黑暗一同升高。你知道，这类景象的咒符有时候非常动人心魄，那夜便是如此。可我觉得，来到我心上的那些东西，与景象的神秘和奇妙并无直接的关系。"

===Original===
"Not merely the subtle transformations of the spirit that come with beauty," put in the doctor, noticing his hesitation.

"Exactly," Vezin went on, duly encouraged and no longer so fearful of our smiles at his expense. "The impressions came from somewhere else. For instance, down the busy main street where men and women were bustling home from work, shopping at stalls and barrows, idly gossiping in groups, and all the rest of it, I saw that I aroused no interest and that no one turned to stare at me as a foreigner and stranger. I was utterly ignored, and my presence among them excited no special interest or attention.

"And then, quite suddenly, it dawned upon me with conviction that all the time this indifference and inattention were merely feigned. Everybody as a matter of fact was watching me closely. Every movement I made was known and observed. Ignoring me was all a pretence﻿—an elaborate pretence."

===Chinese===
"不仅仅是美所带来的心灵上那些微妙的变化。"医生注意到他的迟疑，便接了一句。

"正是，"维赞继续说，得到了应有的鼓励，不再那么怕我们笑话他。"那些感受来自别的什么地方。比方说，在那条繁忙的大街上，男男女女正忙着下班回家，在摊位和手推车前采购，三三两两闲聊，如此种种——我看见我引不起任何人的兴趣，没有谁回过头来盯着我这个外国人、这个陌生人看。我被彻底忽视了，我的存在在他们中间没有激起任何特别的兴趣或注意。

"然后，非常突然地，我带着一种确信恍然大悟：一直以来，这种冷漠和漫不经心不过是装出来的。事实上，每一个人都在密切注视着我。我的每一个动作都被知晓、被观察着。忽视我全是假装的——一场精心编排的假装。"

===Original===
He paused a moment and looked at us to see if we were smiling, and then continued, reassured﻿—

"It is useless to ask me how I noticed this, because I simply cannot explain it. But the discovery gave me something of a shock. Before I got back to the inn, however, another curious thing rose up strongly in my mind and forced my recognition of it as true. And this, too, I may as well say at once, was equally inexplicable to me. I mean I can only give you the fact, as fact it was to me."

The little man left his chair and stood on the mat before the fire. His diffidence lessened from now onwards, as he lost himself again in the magic of the old adventure. His eyes shone a little already as he talked.

===Chinese===
他停了片刻，望了望我们，看我们是否在笑，然后又安下心来接着说——

"问我怎么注意到这些，是没用的，因为我实在解释不了。可这一发现还是给了我一点震动。不过，在我回到客栈之前，又有一桩古怪的事在脑海中强烈地浮现出来，迫使我承认它是真的。而这件事呢，我也索性直说了吧，对我来说同样不可解释。我的意思是，我只能把事实给你，因为它于我就是事实。"

小个子离开椅子，站到炉前的毡垫上。从这时起，他的怯意开始消退，因为他又一次沉浸到那桩旧日冒险的魔法中去了。他说着话，眼睛已经微微发起光来。

===Original===
